(3.29)

We now proceed with the dimensional reduction by setting
\mathchoice{T^{{{t}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\theta}}}}{T^{{{t}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\theta}}}}{T^{{{t}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\theta}}}}{T^{{{t}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\theta}}}}=0=\mathchoice{T^{{{\theta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{T^{{{\theta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{T^{{{\theta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{T^{{{\theta}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}
and we Wick rotate
(\tau=it)
to match the notation of
[
42
]
. Combining everything, the flow equation (
1.1
) takes the following form

\displaystyle\partialderivative{S_{\text{E}}}{\la'}=\int\differential{\tau}\sqrt{\gamma}\hskip 2.84544pt\dfrac{T^{2}-b_{2}(1+b_{2})/{\lambda^{\prime}}^{2}}{1+2b_{2}-2T\lambda^{\prime}}\,,

(3.30)

where
T\coloneqq\mathchoice{T^{{{\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\tau}}}}{T^{{{\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\tau}}}}{T^{{{\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\tau}}}}{T^{{{\tau}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\tau}}}}
, assuming that the metric determinant has no spacial dependence. The deformation above was derived using the Wheeler-DeWitt method
[
42
,
38
]
and it is instructive to compare the two approaches. Briefly, one begins by considering the following dilaton gravity theory

\displaystyle S_{\text{E}}=-\dfrac{1}{2\kappa^{2}}\int_{M}\differential[2]{x}\sqrt{g}\hskip 2.84544pt\{\Phi R+2U(\Phi)\}-\dfrac{1}{\kappa^{2}}\int_{\partial M}\differential{\tau}\sqrt{\mathchoice{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tau}{\tau}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tau}{\tau}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tau}{\tau}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tau}{\tau}}}}}\hskip 2.84544pt\Phi\{K-1\}\,,

(3.31)

where
\mathchoice{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tau}{\tau}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tau}{\tau}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tau}{\tau}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68787pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.14204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tau}{\tau}}}}
is the boundary metric and
K
measures the extrinsic curvature. Now, using the methodology developed in
[
38
]
, the authors of
[
42
]
derive the following flow equation

\displaystyle\partialderivative{S_{\text{EFT}}}{\la}=\int\differential{\tau}\sqrt{\gamma}\hskip 2.84544pt\dfrac{T^{2}-(1-(r_{c}\Phi_{r})^{-1}U(r_{c}\Phi_{r}))/(4\lambda)^{2}}{1/2-2\lambda T}\,,

(3.32)

where
\Phi_{r}\coloneqq\Phi/r
and
r_{c}
is the finite radial cut-off which is a function of
\lambda
.

Assuming now that this flow equation holds even when
r_{c}
is independent of
\lambda
and carefully comparing (
3.30
) with (
3.32
), we are lead to a yet another constraint, and an interesting identification, i.e.

\displaystyle b_{2}=\dfrac{1}{2}(b_{0}-1)\,,\qquad b_{0}\longleftrightarrow\pm\sqrt{\dfrac{r_{c}\Phi_{r}}{U(r_{c}\Phi_{r})}}\,.

(3.33)

To be more precise, in order for the identification to be valid in general, one is obligated to perform exactly the same uplift as in (
3.24
). Since
U(x)
is a potential term and because we assumed that
r_{c}
does not depend on the deformation parameter, the criteria for the uplift are precisely satisfied. From there it follows that for the
\text{AdS}_{2}
potential, that is
U(x)=x
(where the assumption for the radial cut-off is redundant), we trivially retrieve
b_{0}=1
and
b_{2}=0=b_{3}
which reduces the operator (
3.12
) to
T\overline{T}
as expected, as well as
b_{0}=-1=b_{2}
and
b_{3}=0
which again reduces the operator to
T\overline{T}
for seed conformal field theories using (
3.27
). Finally, it is worthwhile to point out that there does exist a generalization of (
3.32
) assuming additional matter to be present
[
42
]
. Our extension (
3.30
) cannot match the extra matter terms that appear, meaning that, potentially, one needs to begin from the more general solution (
3.1
) where
b_{2}
and
b_{3}
are independent. Then, all
\mathfrak{c_{i}}
have to be fixed by introducing the dimensionful couplings
\mathfrak{g}_{i}
indicating the existence of new matter terms, in agreement with the aforementioned generalization. For the next step, the new trace-flow equation must be found via (
2.6
) and (
2.9
) with the dimensional reduction following in exactly the same way as before.
7
7

7

Please do note that since more couplings are now present, the trace of the undeformed stress-tensor may not be zero, which is exactly the source of new matter.
Lastly, the two approaches are compared side by side, yielding constraints and identifications similar to the ones above. It would be interesting to investigate under which conditions for
b_{0}
and
b_{2}
, this identification holds when
r_{c}
is a function of
\lambda
.

Let us now study the flow equation (
3.30
). The Lorentzian version of the deforming operator reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}=\int\differential{t}f\hskip 1.42271pt2b_{0}\dfrac{T^{2}-b_{2}(1+b_{2})/{\lambda^{\prime}}^{2}}{1+2b_{2}-2T\lambda^{\prime}}\,,

(3.34)

where
T\coloneqq\mathchoice{T^{{{t}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{T^{{{t}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{T^{{{t}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}{T^{{{t}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{t}}}}
. Given this deformation, we can derive the corresponding massive gravity action and deform a theory classically. Since we are now living in one dimension, both einbeins are equal to their determinants. Similarly to (
3.3
), we now proceed with a slightly different ansatz

\displaystyle G(\lambda^{\prime},\mathfrak{g}_{i},y_{1})=\dfrac{c_{0}(\lambda^{\prime},\mathfrak{g}_{i})}{y_{1}}+c_{1}(\lambda^{\prime},\mathfrak{g}_{i})+c_{2}(\lambda^{\prime},\mathfrak{g}_{i})y_{1}\,,

(3.35)

therefore, from (
2.7
) and (
3.34
) we retrieve the following massive gravity solution

\displaystyle S_{\text{G}}=\int\differential{t}\dfrac{f}{\lambda^{\prime}}\quantity{\dfrac{1}{2}+b_{2}-\dfrac{\mathfrak{c_{0}}(\mathfrak{g}_{i})}{4y_{1}}-\dfrac{y_{1}}{4\mathfrak{c_{0}}(\mathfrak{g}_{i})}+\la'y_{1}\mathfrak{c_{2}}(\mathfrak{g}_{i})}\,,

(3.36)

where the units of
\mathfrak{c_{i}}
are

\displaystyle\quantity[\mathfrak{c_{0}}(\mathfrak{g}_{i})]=M^{0}\,,\qquad\quantity[\mathfrak{c_{2}}(\mathfrak{g}_{i})]=M^{1}\,.

(3.37)

The last ingredient that we will need is the seed function, which we choose to be a non-linear sigma model of bosons and fermions interacting with an arbitrary potential
V
plus topological terms which are denoted by
\mathcal{T}
. The explicit action reads

\displaystyle S_{0}=\int\differential{t}e\hskip 1.42271pt\quantity{\dfrac{1}{2e^{2}}\tensor{A}{_{i}_{j}}\dot{\phi}^{i}\dot{\phi}^{j}\mp\dfrac{i}{2e}\tensor{B}{_{i}_{j}}\qty(\bar{\psi}^{i}\dot{\psi}^{j}-\dot{\bar{\psi}}^{i}\psi^{j})-V+\dfrac{\mathcal{T}}{e}}\,,

(3.38)

where
i,j
are the flavour indices. There are two points in the equation above that we need to stress. First, it is clear that fermions behave exactly like the topological term in terms of their interaction with the background, in other words they decouple completely. Second, we understand that in reality we are not necessarily concerned about the exact structure of the matter fields, but only about the way they couple to the background. To make things more precise, consider the following definitions

\displaystyle\Phi\coloneqq\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}\dot{\phi}^{i}\dot{\phi}^{j}\,,\qquad\Psi\coloneqq\pm i\mathchoice{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}\quantity(\bar{\psi}^{i}\dot{\psi}^{j}-\dot{\bar{\psi}}^{i}\psi^{j})\,,

(3.39)

which brings the seed action to a more convenient form

\displaystyle S_{0}=\int\differential{t}e\hskip 1.42271pt\quantity{\dfrac{1}{2e^{2}}\Phi-\dfrac{1}{2e}\qty(\Psi-2\mathcal{T})-V}\,.

(3.40)

This simple rewriting essentially allows one to deform not just bosons and fermions but any matter action that couples to the background in the same way. This is one of the key advantages of the massive gravity method and the main reason for which these type of deformations are usually referred as purely “geometrical”. We now combine (
3.36
,
3.40
) and solve the equation of motion for
e
which gives

\displaystyle\bar{e}_{\pm}=\pm f\sqrt{\dfrac{\mathfrak{c_{0}}\quantity(\mathfrak{c_{0}}-2\la'\Phi/f^{2})}{1+4\lambda^{\prime}\mathfrak{c_{0}}(V-\mathfrak{c_{2}})}}\,,

(3.41)

with the deformed action following

\displaystyle S_{\mp}=\int\differential{t}f\quantity{\dfrac{1+2b_{2}\mp\sqrt{\qty[1-2\la'\Phi/(\mathfrak{c_{0}}f^{2})]\qty[1+4\la'\mathfrak{c_{0}}(V-\mathfrak{c_{2}})]}}{2\la'}-\dfrac{1}{2f}\qty(\Psi-2\mathcal{T})}.

(3.42)

Now applying the initial condition (
2.8
) gives
\mathfrak{c_{0}}=1
,
\mathfrak{c_{2}}=0
and
b_{2}=0
, and for these choices, the theory above matches exactly the one given in
[
42
]
.

Finally, the action above can be brought in the form of a relativistic particle for the static gauge choice when the fermions and the topological term are turned off. In order to do this we define the proper time and a target space metric as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}\coloneqq\int_{0}^{t}f(\tilde{t})\differential{\tilde{t}}\,,\qquad\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}\coloneqq\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{0}{0}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\m (3.43)

and proceed with the following identification

\displaystyle m^{-1}\longleftrightarrow 2\lambda^{\prime}\,,\quad\dot{X}^{i}\longleftrightarrow\sqrt{2\lambda^{\prime}}\dot{\phi}^{i}\,,\quad\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}\longleftrightarrow\quantity[1+4\la'\mathfrak{c_{0}}(V-\mathfrak{c_{2}})]\quantity[-1\oplus\tensor{A}{_{i}_{j}}/\mathfrak{c_{0}}]\,,

(3.44)

where
m
is the mass of the particle and the derivatives are now with respect to proper time. This leads to the following action

\displaystyle S_{\mp}=\mp\int\differential{\mathfrak{t}}m\sqrt{-\dot{X}^{\mu}\dot{X}^{\nu}\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}}\,,\qquad\dot{X}^{0}=1\,,

(3.45)

for
b_{2}=-1/2
. We should note that the mass can be taken out of the integral only when
b_{0}
is not a function of proper time. During the next subsection we will repeat the same process but in two dimensions.

3.5
Sigma models

After the one-dimensional detour, we are back to two dimensions and we would like to apply the massive gravity method to the same model as before, i.e. a non-linear sigma model of bosons and fermions interacting with an arbitrary potential plus topological terms. This has been done multiple times in the past using different approaches
[
3
,
27
,
4
,
5
,
6
,
8
]
, but we aim to provide a more transparent expression for the deformed action. Let us begin from the seed action which reads

\displaystyle S_{0}=\int\differential[2]{x}e\hskip 1.42271pt\quantity{-\dfrac{1}{2}\tensor{g}{^{\mu}^{\nu}}\tensor{\Phi}{_{\mu}_{\nu}}-\dfrac{1}{2}\tensor{e}{^{\mu}_{a}}\tensor{\Psi}{^{a}_{\mu}}-V+\dfrac{\mathcal{T}}{e}}\,,

where

have similar definitions as before i.e.

(3.47)

Once again, this rewriting allows one to deform any theory that couples in the same way to the background. Proceeding now with the gravity action (
3.11
) and the seed action above, we find the following solution for the auxiliary zweibein

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{(\bar{e}_{\pm})}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{(\bar{e}_{\pm})}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{(\bar{e}_{\pm})}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{(\bar{e}_{\pm})}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=\dfrac{\lambda^{\prime}}{2\widetilde{\lambda^{\prime}}}\quantity(\tensor{\widehat{f}}{_{\mu}^{a}}\pm\dfrac{\tensor{\widehat{f}}{_{\mu}^{a}}+2\widetilde{\la'}\tensor{\Phi}{_{\mu}_{\nu}}\tensor{\widehat{f}}{^{\nu}_{b}}\tensor{\eta}{^{b}^{a}}}{\sqrt{\widehat{\g}^{-1}\det(\tensor{\widehat{\g}}{_{\mu}_{\nu}}+2\widetilde{\la'}\tensor{\Phi}{_{\mu}_{\nu}})}})\,,

(3.48)

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda^{\prime}}\coloneqq\lambda^{\prime}(1-V\lambda^{\prime})\,,\qquad\mathchoice{\widehat{f}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\widehat{f}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\widehat{f}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\widehat{f}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\coloneqq\mathchoice{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005 (3.49)

For the case of a single scalar boson, without fermions, the solution above reduces to the one found in
[
8
]
. Using (
3.48
) we obtain the following deformed action

\displaystyle S_{\pm}=\int\differential[2]{x}f\hskip 1.42271pt\quantity{\dfrac{1+b_{2}}{\la'}-\dfrac{1}{2\widetilde{\la'}f}\qty(\sqrt{-\det(\tensor{\widehat{\g}}{_{\mu}_{\nu}})}\pm\sqrt{-\det(\tensor{\widehat{\g}}{_{\mu}_{\nu}}+2\widetilde{\la'}\tensor{\Phi}{_{\mu}_{\nu}})})+\dfrac{\mathcal{T}}{f}}\,.

(3.50)

There are certain comments that need to be made regarding the action above. First, the expression agrees with (
C.28
), which was derived using the ansatz method with the fermions turned off. Furthermore, for
b_{2}=0
the action agrees with the ones derived in
[
5
,
6
]
and consequently with
[
3
,
27
,
4
,
8
]
, hence its validity is secured. Moreover we observe something interesting when the bosons and potential are absent. The solution (
3.48
) now reads

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{(\bar{e}_{+})}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{(\bar{e}_{+})}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{(\bar{e}_{+})}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{{(\bar{e}_{+})}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=\mathchoice{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\d le$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\Psi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,,

(3.51)

which is linear in
\lambda^{\prime}
. This is important because in that limit, the deformed action is also linear in
\lambda^{\prime}
. Considering these facts, we naturally conclude that the source of this linear dependence comes entirely from the way fermions couple to the background and not due to their anti-commuting nature. This is consistent with the fact that
\mathchoice{\Psi^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\Psi^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\Psi^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\Psi^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}
may represent any matter field with this index structure. In other words, visiting (
3.46
), one notices that
\mathchoice{\Psi^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\Psi^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\Psi^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\Psi^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}
couples linearly to the background, therefore the deformed action will also be linear but now in
\lambda^{\prime}
. This answers a question posed by the authors of
[
6
]
.

Finally, we observe that the square root structures above for a flat background, can be interpreted as Nambu-Goto densities up to certain identifications for the target space coordinates and string tension
[
3
]
, with
\mathchoice{\widehat{\gamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\widehat{\gamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\widehat{\gamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\widehat{\gamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}
and
\mathchoice{\widehat{\gamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\widehat{\gamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\widehat{\gamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\widehat{\gamma}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}+2\widetilde{\lambda^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\Phi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\Phi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\Phi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\Phi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}
being the world-sheet metrics respectively. Turning off the potential and topological terms makes this identification more precise in a way that it is now consistent with the spectrum analysis in subsection
3.2
. In more detail, the choice
b_{2}=-1/2
once again kills the constant term in (
3.50
) as expected.

3.6
Scalar boson of order
k

Given our results, we would like to explore how higher-derivative theories are deformed. For simplicity, consider the conformal scalar boson of order
k
as a seed theory, temporarily in
d
dimensions. Our methodology is valid only for theories that couple to a curved background, which in our case translates to

\displaystyle S_{0}=\dfrac{1}{2}\int\differential[d]{x}\phi\Box^{k}\phi\longrightarrow\dfrac{1}{2}\int\differential[d]{x}e\hskip 1.42271pt\quantity{\phi\Box^{k}\phi+\text{curvature terms}}\,,

(3.52)

where the curvature terms can be fixed exactly for any
k\in\mathds{N}^{+}

[
54
,
55
,
56
,
57
]
. There are two issues with this approach. First, curvature couplings are introduced, therefore the second derivative of the auxiliary vielbein will appear, which in turn means that finding the vielbein solution to the equations of motion for
S_{\text{G}}+S_{0}
is improbable. The second and more serious problem arises due to the dependence of the curvature terms on
d
. For example, at
k=2
there are curvature terms that carry coefficients which are proportional to
(d-2)^{n}
where
n
is negative
[
58
]
, hence one cannot simply set
d=2
.

To avoid these issues entirely we will attack the problem from a different angle. Let us return to two dimensions where we will introduce
(k-1)
auxiliary scalar fields
[
59
,
60
]
and consider the following action

\displaystyle S_{0}=\int\differential[2]{x}e\hskip 1.42271pt\quantity{-\dfrac{1}{2}\tensor{g}{^{\mu}^{\nu}}\tensor{A}{_{i}_{j}}\partial_{(\mu}\phi^{i}\partial_{\nu)}\phi^{j}-\dfrac{1}{2}\tensor{B}{_{i}_{j}}\phi^{i}\phi^{j}-\mathcal{V}(\phi^{k},\mathfrak{g}_{i})}\,,\qquad 1\leq i,j\leq k\,,

(3.53)

where the matrices
A
and
B
are given by

\displaystyle\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\cdots&0&1\\
\vdots&\rotatebox{10.0}{$\iddots$}&\rotatebox{10.0}{$\iddots$}&0\\
0&\rotatebox{10.0}{$\iddots$}&\rotatebox{10.0}{$\iddots$}&\vdots\\
1&0&\cdots&0\end{pmatrix}\,,\qquad\mathchoice{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\cdots&0&1&\omega\\
\vdots&\rotatebox{10.0}{$\iddots$}&\rotatebox{10.0}{$\iddots$}&\rotatebox{10.0}{$\iddots$}&0\\
0&\rotatebox{10.0}{$\iddots$}&\rotatebox{10.0}{$\iddots$}&\rotatebox{10.0}{$\iddots$}&\vdots\\
1&\rotatebox{10.0}{$\iddots$}&\rotatebox{10.0}{$\iddots$}&\rotatebox{10.0}{$\iddots$}&\vdots\\
\omega&0&\cdots&\cdots&0\end{pmatrix}\,,

(3.54)

with
\omega
being an arbitrary dimensionless constant. The equations of motion for this model are
[
59
,
60
]

\displaystyle\quantity(\Box-\omega)\phi^{1}=\partialderivative{\mathcal{V}}{\phi^{k}}\,,\qquad\quantity(\Box-\omega)\phi^{i}=\phi^{i-1}\,,\qquad 2\leq i\leq k\,,

(3.55)

thus, using these we can eliminate the first
(k-1)
fields, leaving us with an action that depends only on
\phi^{k}
, which we will rename to
\phi
for simplicity. Then, for
\omega=0
we reach

\displaystyle S_{0}=\int\differential[2]{x}e\hskip 1.42271pt\quantity{\dfrac{1}{2}\phi\Box^{k}\phi-\mathcal{V}(\phi,\mathfrak{g}_{i})}\,.

(3.56)

One notices that essentially, the role of the auxiliary fields is to maximally reduce the order of the derivatives. In our case, the reduced action is a simple sigma model plus a potential, which can be deformed easily using (
3.50
) and setting

\displaystyle V=-\dfrac{1}{2}\mathchoice{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{B^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}\phi^{i}\phi^{j}-\mathcal{V}\,,

(3.57)

where fermions and topological terms have been turned off. From there, one has two choices: either attempt to integrate out the auxiliary scalar fields assuming that the two processes commute, or work exclusively with (
3.53
) where the number of derivatives is under control. We propose that the latter may be more productive, as any attempt to integrate out the auxiliary fields, even in the simplest case, did not produce any useful outcome. We should also note that the addition of topological terms might no longer be trivial since they may depend on the scalar fields, which in turn will affect the equations of motion. Finally, this approach may not be unique only to scalar fields and it would be interesting to study the generalisation to fields with higher spin.

3.7
Root deformations

The final stop in this two-dimensional journey is the study of the root-
T\overline{T}
deformation from the massive gravity perspective. The marginal operator that generates this deformation is given by
[
20
]

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}=\int\differential[2]{x}f\hskip 1.42271pt\dfrac{b_{\text{r}}}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}\mathchoice{T^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}-\frac{1}{2}(\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}})^{2}}\,,

(3.58)

where
b_{\text{r}}
is a dimensionless constant. We will attach the letter
\mu
to the deformation parameter for the operator above. Proceeding as usual, equation (
2.7
) for this choice becomes

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}G=b_{\text{r}}\sqrt{2y_{2}-y_{1}^{2}}(\partial_{y_{1}}G+2y_{1}\partial_{y_{2}}G)\,,

(3.59)

where
b_{\text{r}}
has absorbed a
\pm
factor, that is equation (
C.25
) precisely. This is interesting. If we consider the approach we followed in subsection
C.3
, the flow equation for the deformed Lagrangian density was derived via a sigma model seed theory ansatz which is entirely constructed via scalar fields. In contrast, the same flow equation is derived for
G
, which is also a Lagrangian density, but now the building blocks are zweibeins and even more so, we have not specified the seed theory. This poses as a strong indicator towards the fact that root-
T\overline{T}
deformations are universal and independent of background-matter couplings, which in turn means that they cannot have a massive gravity description, at least not in the same way as the deformations that admit one. We can actually provide a more concrete argument about this fact, by solving the equation above. Let us temporarily forget that the solutions can be brought in the form of (
C.26
,
C.27
) and solve (
3.59
) from scratch. We find

\displaystyle G(\mu,\mathfrak{g}_{i},y_{1},y_{2})=\mathcall{G}\quantity[\mathfrak{g}_{i},y_{1}^{2}-y_{2},\mu b_{\text{r}}+\arctanh\qty(y_{1}^{-1}\sqrt{2y_{2}-y_{1}^{2}})]\,,

(3.60)

where
\mathcall{G}
is any function. We immediately notice that the deformation parameter decouples completely from
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
in the arguments of the solution. Moreover, applying the initial condition (
2.8
) with
\mu\rightarrow 0
does not uniquely fix
G
, yet a choice that is consistent reads

\displaystyle G(\mu,\mathfrak{g}_{i},y_{1},y_{2})=\mathcall{F}(\mathfrak{g}_{i})\quantity(y_{1}^{2}-y_{2}+\mu b_{\text{r}}+\arctanh\qty(y_{1}^{-1}\sqrt{2y_{2}-y_{1}^{2}})-2)\,,

(3.61)

where
\mathcall{F}
is an arbitrary dimensionless function. Since any solution that satisfies (
2.8
) should provide a massive gravity description and a counterexample exists, we conclude that (
3.58
) cannot have a massive gravity description on its own. This behaviour is reminiscent of gauge symmetry, in the way that it should work for any gauge choice. Nevertheless, the alternative interpretation is that the initial condition suggests that a massive gravity density is already present, possibly due to other deformations, and the effect of the root-
T\overline{T}
is the additional structure given by the solution above. Considering this, let us now write down the more useful form of the solution
[
20
]
i.e. (
C.26
,
C.27
) or

\displaystyle G(\mu,\mathfrak{g}_{i},y_{1},y_{2})=\mathcall{G}\quantity(\mathfrak{g}_{i},y_{1,\m},y_{2,\m})\,,

(3.62)

where

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &y_{1,\mu}=y_{1}\cosh(b_{\text{r}}\m)+\sqrt{2y_{2}-y_{1}^{2}}\sinh(b_{\text{r}}\m)\,,\\
&y_{2,\mu}=y_{2}\cosh(2b_{\text{r}}\m)+y_{1}\sqrt{2y_{2}-y_{1}^{2}}\sinh(2b_{\text{r}}\m)\,,\end{aligned}

(3.63)

and the initial condition has been arbitrarily (but very conveniently) set at
\mu=0
, where
\mathcall{G}\quantity(\mathfrak{g}_{i},y_{1,\m},y_{2,\m}){|}_{\mu=0}
reduces to the “seed” gravity action
\mathcall{G}\quantity(\mathfrak{g}_{i},y_{1},y_{2})
. Interestingly, the second argument in (
3.60
) indicates that
y_{2,\mu}-y_{1,\mu}^{2}=y_{2}-y_{1}^{2}
, which is verified by the set of equations above.

Combining all of the above we understand that in order to deform exclusively with root-
T\overline{T}
we have to follow a very specific procedure. First, we need to turn on a deformation, say with
\lambda
as a coupling, that admits a massive gravity solution. Then, using equations (
3.62
) and (
3.63
), we introduce the root deformation as discussed and integrate out the auxiliary zweibein in order to “push” both deformations on the seed theory. Finally, we need to turn off
\lambda
to remove the effect of the extra deformation. Actually, since the root-
T\overline{T}
structure is unaffected by the “seed” massive gravity, the choice one makes originally for a deformation does not matter. We can actually take things a step further by taking advantage of that fact, and provide some more insight about root-
T\overline{T}
.

Let us consider the solution (
3.62
) and a seed action as usual. Then their combination gives the deformed theory (
3.1
)

\displaystyle
